[Image]

Team USA’s peacekeeper pipe dream just got spookier. In his [Oval Office séance](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=2mrUGOSEMGc), Donald cranked up the confusion to eleven, promising Vova's time to "get smart" is running out while offering zero details on how he'll actually squeeze Russia. When pressed on whether Vova's a liar, Donald said he'd "find out"—peak mystification achieved.

[Video: https://youtu.be/X8FIRt7mASM]

Little Marco feeds a dangerous tendency: We want so badly to believe America will again stand for what it used to, will soon be admired as it once was, that we overinterpret every hint that peace is summoning itself. This hopeful blindness makes us marks, distracting us from the hard fate being conjured for Ukraine under this administration's watch.

Step back from Donald's spectacular hokum, and Vova's real strategy materializes: he's hunched over a geopolitical ouija board, channeling two dark spirits—nuclear annihilation and fleeting time.

As practicing philosopher Vladimir Pastukhov points out, in a full NATO war, Russia could summon apocalypse but can't conjure victory. Without nukes, Russia loses guaranteed. This isn't 1962, and Russia isn't the USSR, despite Kremlin séances trying to resurrect dead empires. The tech gap is too wide for any medium to bridge[1](#footnote-1).

But Vova's planchette has found two spectral opportunities moving across the board:

**Space**: The West has blurred boundaries between those within Europe's pale and those condemned to wander beyond it. For centuries, pales have enclosed the "area that is safe"—Dublin's Pale, Calais's Pale, Russia's Pale of Settlement. To be beyond the pale means being outside the area accepted as "home." Ukraine became the first tragic example of what happens when a nation is kept outside NATO's protective fence, caught between sham democracy and Vova's imperial designs. Now the Baltic specter is materializing. Moscow's ouija board spells out opportunity wherever the West won't clearly invoke "this territory is within our pale—we'll die for it."

*If there is a pale, decent people stay inside it* —John Harington, 1657.

**Time**: The West dozed off after the post-Cold War honeymoon and let its protective wards weaken (except America). Donald's budget exorcism came late. Europe's military resurrection will banish Russian phantoms—in five or six years. Until then, Moscow's séance continues.

Vova's channeling this message to Donald: summon chaos on the periphery fast, manifest massive damage, then face the spirits later. Donald calls these Vova's "supernatural forces." The West hasn't figured out its counter-ritual, hoping Vova's nerve will fail him. But for Vova, losing Ukraine costs more than risking communion with darker powers.

**The West's Exorcisms:**

1. **Full invocation**: Declare the farthest borderlands worth defending with every available spirit. Problem: Moscow has to believe your séance is real.
2. **Use Ukraine as the medium**: Either cut a deal that puts Russia's war machine into spiritual slumber (hoping they can't wake the dead fast enough), or keep Ukraine's resistance strong enough that Russia can't summon a Baltic front without calling up mass mobilization spirits.

Donald and Europe and Z are fighting over which ritual to perform, while Vova's planchette keeps moving across the board, spelling out his next move—even as Donald promised he'd "find out" what that move might be.

## Day 1266

*Date: 2025-08-12 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Pass the pixie dust*

[Image]

Not much left of [Bilozerske](https://deepstatemap.live/#12/48.5044360/37.0816040), population 14,000, where it has been raining KAB bombs for the last couple of days. KAB is short for Korrektiruyemaya Aviatsionnaya Bomba, which translates to "Correctable Aerial Bomb." These are Soviet-era bombs upgraded with extendable wings and a guidance system. Russian pilots launch them at the edge of Ukrainian airspace and fly home as the bomb glides toward its target.

---

I almost regretted comparing Donald to Peter Pan and Vova to Captain Hook the other day, imagining that both were walking the plank over Ukraine. But not after Donald announced what promises to be the most surreal real estate transaction since Manhattan was bought for beads.

Donald claims he'll know "in the first two minutes" whether a deal can be made—the same keen dealmaking intuition that led him to confidently promise [53 times](https://www.perplexity.ai/search/how-many-times-did-donald-trum-Ip6CO4EQSoWQIrEZk1UPJA#0) during the campaign that he'd end Russia's genocidal war in 24 hours. The solution? Vova gets to play gracious guest in Alaska on August 15 by accepting an "invitation" to American soil, which Donald finds "very respectful"—rather like finding it respectful when Captain Hook drops by your treehouse.

The [transcript](https://www.rev.com/transcripts/trump-details-crime-crackdown-for-d-c) of Donald's media circus yesterday reads like a fever dream, but the Ukraine portions are pure real estate pornography. He casually mentions "land swapping" and notes that Russia has grabbed "largely... oceanfront property" because, as any good mogul knows, waterfront is always prime. One can almost hear him appraising Crimea: "Beautiful location, great views, recently renovated by new management."

Most deliciously absurd is Donald's complaint that Z requires "constitutional approval" for territorial concessions—imagine that, a democracy requiring democratic processes!—while simultaneously boasting that he personally "stopped the biggest pipeline in the world" and gave Ukraine "tank busters" instead of Obama's "sheets." It's Peter Pan bragging about both saving and abandoning the Lost Boys, depending on which sounds more heroic.

The whole affair reeks of two larger than life jerks who've painted themselves into their respective corners. Vova, having bitten off more Ukrainian territory than he can apparently digest, suddenly discovers diplomacy. TACO Donald, having promised to end the war in a day, now speaks of "feel out meetings" and "complex" situations requiring multiple European consultations.

When Donald mentions there will be "some swapping" of territory and describes Russia's conquests as "very prime territory... oceanfront property," we're witnessing something unprecedented: a real estate developer treating sovereign nations like a Monopoly board while millions await their fate.

The real tragedy isn't that Peter Pan and Captain Hook are meeting in Alaska—it's that they're discussing Ukrainian lives and territory as if it were just another ratings opportunity. Pass the pixie dust—we're all going to need it.

## Day 1267

*Date: 2025-08-13 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*The 107-year itch*

[Image]

[The Treaty of Brest-Litovsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Brest-Litovsk) delivered Ukraine its first taste of 20th century great power "independence": Lenin agreed to stop fighting, the Germans moved in as liberators, then came rapid collapse and civil war chaos. Now we're watching the sequel, with Ukraine once more caught between great powers deciding its fate without meaningful Ukrainian input.

Donald's recent press conference announcing his Anchorage summit was a masterclass in amplifying Russian propaganda—all while insisting he's the toughest negotiator Vova has ever faced. Meanwhile, Z's latest pep talks sound like whistling past the graveyard: "No decisions will be made on Ukraine without Ukraine." The real question is whether Ukraine gets a voice in the negotiations or just gets to rubber-stamp whatever these two hash out in Anchorage.

**Team USA's Warm-Up Act**

The most revealing moment came when Donald channeled Viktor Orbán to explain Russian military doctrine. According to his Hungarian whisperer, Russia always wins because "they fight wars. That's what they do." This historical determinism conveniently ignores Russia's spectacular losses in 1905, 1917, 1979, and 2022. But why let facts interfere with a good invincivity narrative?

Donald's role as Vova's real estate agent was equally striking. He gushed over Russia's territorial acquisitions, describing occupied Ukrainian lands as "very prime territory" and coveted "oceanfront property." This wasn't analysis—it was a sales pitch reframing war crimes as savvy property investment.

The blame-shifting was pure Kremlin gold. Vova bears no responsibility for Europe's largest war since 1945; it's "Joe Biden's war." Vova was just an innocent bystander swept along by American incompetence—a narrative that strips him of agency while absolving moral responsibility.

Donald's advance team has already warmed up Vova's negotiating position. As JD declared on Fox News: "The President and I certainly think that America is done with the funding of the Ukraine war business." Nothing says tough negotiation like announcing your red lines before you even sit down.

**The Ukrainian Equation**

Whatever Donald and Vova decide, Ukraine will swallow. The only variable is how much Z will have to smile while doing it. He might manage to stomach dividing Ukraine along current battle lines, though explaining that to Ukrainians won't exactly be a career highlight. What he absolutely cannot do is voluntarily hand over territories Russia hasn't yet conquered.

Not yet, anyway.

Ukraine's surrender formula simply hasn't ripened. Three possible endings await: Z could solve his mobilization crisis and deliver a knockout punch, inspiring Europe to double down on a post-American war effort. Or the counterattack could fail spectacularly, forcing Z into his own Brest-Litovsk moment—signing away everything he'd previously died defending. Or Ukraine could collapse so completely that Vova's appetite grows beyond current territories.

The catch? To survive such capitulation, you need Lenin's revolutionary ruthlessness—the ability to sacrifice territory for survival, then spend decades plotting revenge. Z lacks this cold calculation. He appears too invested in self-aggrandizement to make the brutal pivot from hero to realist.

**The Anchorage Sideshow**

So Donald and Vova head to their Alaskan "showdown" not to settle anything, but to size each other up and sketch the rough outlines of Ukraine's eventual partition. This isn't diplomacy; it's reconnaissance with vodka.

Vova doesn't need Donald to be a conscious Russian agent—just a useful one. Donald's attraction to strongmen, transactional view of alliances,and desperate need for diplomatic "wins" make him the perfect mark. The Russian president gets American legitimization of his conquests wrapped in dealmaking language.

They won't reach any deals now (unless Russia's military suddenly discovers competence in the next 48 hours). But they'll absolutely draw dotted lines on all their Molotov-Ribbentrop fantasy maps, saving precious time when circumstances finally force Ukraine's hand.

The tragedy isn't that Donald might strike a bad deal—it's that he's already advertising Ukrainian surrender terms while calling it tough negotiation. It's geopolitical meal prep: chopping all the vegetables now so you can cook quickly when the moment's right.

History doesn't repeat, but it sure does rhyme. And right now, it's rhyming in Russian.

Our bloody little war is stuck on infinite respawn mode—and everyone's pretending the Putin-Trump beano might change something. It won't, because America's chief negotiator fired everyone who might explain why. So, we're left with making threats while admitting powerlessness, claiming toughness while showing reluctance to actually use available tools.

Donald, who decides foreign policy in "two minutes" based on "instinct," meets Vova, who's ruled Russia for 25 years and is "skilled at catching his interlocutors off guard." Donald's top Russia expert? Real estate developer [Steve Witkoff](https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/putin-via-trumps-envoy-witkoff-024751411.html). Vova brings a quarter-century of power and unchanged demands for complete Ukrainian capitulation.

Donald now promises "very severe consequences" if Vova isn't "serious about ending the war"—without specifying what those might be. When asked if he could convince Vova to stop targeting civilians, Donald's answer was refreshingly honest: "I guess the answer to that is probably no."

The Financial Times [reveals](https://archive.ph/UnXEv#selection-1889.0-2555.152) stunning institutional vandalism: Donald fired 1,300 State Department officials last month, including Russia analysts. Twenty-five percent of foreign service officers quit since January. The NSC was "drastically pared back." As former Ambassador Eric Rubin notes: "Donald does not have a single policymaking person who knows Russia and Ukraine advising him."

Meanwhile, the actual war grinds on. ISW [reports](https://understandingwar.org/backgrounder/russian-offensive-campaign-assessment-august-13-2025) Russia suffered 60,000 casualties in July alone—Vova's response is moving 27,000 troops to priority sectors and manufacturing 79,000 strike drones this year. Russian forces infiltrate near Pokrovsk while systematically destroying Ukrainian evacuation routes. Ukraine's sole advantage—deep strikes—is precisely what any Donald "air truce" would eliminate.

Tom Cooper's Ukrainian military sources [describe](https://xxtomcooperxx.substack.com/p/Z-shield) systematic military degeneration under Z, with excellent tactical analysis becoming mere "what if" scenarios. Children walk through combat zones to reach evacuation points while 1,150 more need emergency evacuation from Dobropillia.

[Image]

Here's where farce becomes terrifying: Donald openly plans recreating history's worst diplomatic disasters. Steven Erlanger's analysis [captures](https://www.nytimes.com/2025/08/13/world/europe/yalta-trump-putin-meeting-alaska.html) the horror—this is Munich 1938 meets Yalta 1945. Vova calls Anchorage the "new Yalta," positioning himself as Stalin's heir. Except unlike Stalin, who helped defeat fascism, Vova started this war.

Vova demands territories he's never conquered (like Hitler demanding the Sudetenland), while Donald approaches with less preparation than Chamberlain brought to Munich. European leaders extracted promises Donald won't negotiate without Ukraine present, and Donald claims he'll walk away if Vova isn't acting in "good faith"—but without experts, how will he recognize manipulation? Vova has been preparing for this moment for years; Donald is "lowering expectations."

According to ISW, Vova’s demands remain unchanged since June—four Ukrainian oblasts (including unoccupied areas), regime change in Kyiv, NATO rollback to 1997. These aren't negotiations; they're surrender terms dressed as diplomacy. Donald's "very severe consequences" threats ring particularly hollow given his reluctance to increase economic sanctions despite mounting frustration.

The beano in Alaska will produce either meaningless theater or catastrophically bad agreements, because Donald eliminated anyone who could distinguish between them.

Welcome to Groundhog Day, historical edition—where we repeat the past because we fired everyone who remembered it. Vova already won by getting unprepared, historically ignorant Donald to chat about concessions. The adults left the room, taking 80 years of hard-learned lessons with them.

# Two Jackasses, One Map Collection

*Neither side will open the other's doors or ride in the other's vehicles. If 10 US agents are posted outside a meeting room, 10 Russian agents will stand on the other side. Everything is matched body for body, gun for gun.* — [Secret Service Rushes to Alaska City for Trump-Putin Summit](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-08-14/secret-service-rushes-to-make-alaska-city-safe-for-trump-putin?srnd=homepage-europe) (Bloomberg, August 14, 2025)

After years of winking, pinches and strategic silence… anchorage.

Last week, Trump casually swatted away accusations of rolling over for Putin—while handing over concessions Putin couldn't extract through years of warfare. Just like that. No ceremony, no drama.

Putin can show up practically empty-handed. Well, almost. Word is he's packing "historical materials"—geographical maps to prove Ukraine is an "artificial state." No ceasefire offers. No retraction of pseudo-historical fantasies. Just props.

Oh, and strategic nukes, Arctic rare earths, waterfront development projects. The usual.

Meanwhile, Ukraine and Europe's frantic attempts to crash this party? Complete washout. Trump and Putin will chat about whatever strikes their fancy—including Putin's greatest hits of geographical fiction—while everyone else stews.

Tomorrow's meeting is marinated in subjectivity but locked into hard realities nobody can wave away. Putin showing up with a briefcase full of maps like some deranged geography teacher? Pure theatrical flair.

The pre-meeting pronouncements from world leaders? Theater for the home crowds. Diplomatic "sleep tight, all is well." What Z says matters exactly as much as what Trump, Merz, or Starmer says. Putin's got the right instinct: shut up and let everyone assume you're brilliant. Or at least pack visual aids.

Putin's bringing what Russians call "one of the most representative foreign delegations in Russia's history"—Lavrov, Belousov, Siluanov, the works. Trump will match with his own A-team, minus Pete, [who is in the dog house again](https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2025/aug/14/pete-hegseth-pentagon-christian-nationalism), this time for foisting his religious zealotry on American soldiers ordered to police US cities.

## The Real Game

Putin wants his deal—but only with proper Western genuflection. He'll take partial genuflection for now. His ace? Things are heating up but not quite on fire, so he can drag this out for better terms. The nightmare? Trump bails, taking the golden opportunity with him.

Trump's ready to fold, mainly because he's convinced folding isn't folding. This is Biden's mess. Biden takes the loss. Trump wants to trade Ukrainian real estate for Russian friendship—the "more valuable prize." Problem: his people aren't ready to give away all of Ukraine. Tough sell.

Two guys desperately want to consummate this thing, but Ukrainians keep cockblocking them. Tomorrow they'll try to muscle through the obstacles—Putin armed with questionable cartography—but they probably won't have enough runway to close the deal.

We're watching history pivot on Trump’s bullshit and Putin's map collection.

The war grinds on. People die.

Have a great weekend!

[Image]

I took the first metro to nowhere very, very early. Most of my fellow passengers were only half awake.

Three days running (Days 1267, 1268, 1269) I thought about the impending disaster. But even my most cynical predictions couldn't capture the spectacular awfulness that had just emanated from Anchorage.

Three hours of "extremely productive" talks produced zero deals, zero Russian concessions, and one grinning war criminal getting the full presidential treatment. Putin got everything: American legitimacy, global stage, zero accountability, and a joy ride in "the Beast." Trump got to clap enthusiastically while Putin approached down the red carpet like some demented awards ceremony.

Picture this: B-2 bombers—aircraft designed to vaporize Russia—flying overhead while Trump gives Putin friendly arm taps and lets him cruise shotgun in the presidential limo. Putin was "laughing with glee" inside the armored vehicle meant to protect American presidents from threats exactly like Putin.

When reporters shouted "President Putin, will you stop killing civilians?" Putin just shrugged. Classic Vladimir. Meanwhile, Trump—the man who promised to end this war in 24 hours—stood grinning under a "Pursuing Peace" banner.

[The actual transcript](https://www.cbsnews.com/news/transcript-of-what-putin-trump-said-in-alaska/) is even worse than the reporting. Trump's rambling: "We had an extremely productive meeting, and many points were agreed to... One is probably the most significant, but we have a very good chance of getting there. We didn't get there." Translation: We agreed on nothing important.

Putin delivered a masterclass in manipulation, praising Trump's "precious" efforts to understand "this history" while positioning Russia as Ukraine's hurt older brother. He got Trump to validate his greatest hit: "Today, when President Trump is saying that if he was the president back then, there would be no war... I can confirm that." Then came the pièce de résistance—Putin suggesting "Next time in Moscow" and Trump responding "I could see it possibly happening."

Here's where farce meets tragedy: Trump immediately threw the ball to Z. "Now it's really up to President Z to get it done." The master dealmaker just outsourced the job to the guy he previously shook down in the Oval Office. Z is flying to Washington Monday to discuss Trump's botched adventure.

Putin walked away with American validation and a U.S. president declaring Ukraine must now "get it done." His demands remain unchanged—denazification, demilitarization, territorial surrender. Trump walked away with vibes and a "fantastic relationship with Vladimir."

Meanwhile, Z gets to explain to dying Ukrainians why their American patron just gave their invader a legitimacy bath, then commanded them to negotiate directly with their tormentor.

This wasn't Munich 1938—that required actual negotiation. This was watching someone invite the neighborhood psychopath over for dinner, let him trash the place, then announce the victims need to work things out.

We're watching the sequel to Brest-Litovsk, except this time the Americans are helping write the surrender terms. The war grinds on. People die. And Putin just got everything he wanted: respect, recognition, and a red carpet rolled out by the leader of the free world.

Three hours. Zero deals. One diplomatic catastrophe masquerading as "great progress." Welcome to American foreign policy in 2025: making the world safe for authoritarianism, one summit at a time.

> "We cannot shout for years that we are boys of the bulldog breed, and then suddenly pose as gazelles," Bernard Shaw - [Common Sense About the War](https://www.gutenberg.org/files/13635/13635-h/13635-h.htm) (1914)

---

On the war jacket, Russia bombed Sumy, Kharkiv and Odesa last night. Emergency workers are still counting the dead.

“What’s better, capitulation or continuation of the war?” someone asked Vitaliy last night during his [podcast stream.](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VspXpTxcXqU) A no-brainer. Vitaliy, a Jew, said most of his relatives during WWII, more than 90% of them, were rounded up and died in gas chambers or were shot. That’s what occupiers do to civilians without protection after capitulation, he said.

Our appeasement phase — the first two years of Z’s presidency — is over.

[Image]

The *only* way to achieve “a lasting peace” is to punch Putin in the face, repeatedly. American and European leaders are reluctant to do so, because this is dangerous and costs a lot of money.

---

A regular reader commented last week that Ukraine wasn’t being afforded enough *agency*, at least in my recent posts about the bloody mess. This is a subject I’ve thought and written about over the years.

[Video: https://youtu.be/-wz6lhWxo5g]

**The Performance of Agency**

Two years into his presidency, Z began invoking Ukrainian "subjectiveness" (субъектність) to describe Ukraine's foreign policy position. He insisted Ukraine was "definitely becoming more like subjects of political activity."

But almost every time Z invoked this term, he was describing the opposite of reality. True political subjects reshape their environments—they don't spend months lying about special operations, covering up corruption and ignoring invasion warnings.

As I recall, Bellingcat's Christo Grozev watched the June 2021 interview “fifty times,” particularly the Wagnergate bits, and concluded the president meant "sovereignty," not "subjectiveness." The distinction matters: sovereignty can be performed through treaties and ceremonies, but subjectiveness requires actual agency to transform political systems.

George Bernard Shaw would have recognized this pattern immediately. In 1914, he dismantled Britain's pretensions about fighting for Belgian neutrality. Britain had spent a decade militarizing against Germany, then claimed moral outrage when Germany took the bait. "We cannot shout for years that we are boys of the bulldog breed, and then suddenly pose as gazelles," Shaw wrote.

Shaw's insight was devastating because it came from an outsider—an Irishman who understood imperial dynamics without psychological investment in British exceptionalism. He saw what English patriots couldn't: their country was trapped in rivalry patterns with inexorable logic.

On July 12, 2021 Putin produced a [7,000-word treatise](http://en.kremlin.ru/events/president/news/66181) explaining how Russians and Ukrainians “are one people — a single whole.” From that moment Russian propaganda of war has never ceased. Yet when Russian tanks approached Kyiv, Team USA and Europe convinced themselves they were reluctantly defending international law. The performance had become the policy.

Ukraine, meanwhile, was posing like a gazelle. Ukraine officials dismissed unfavorable intelligence as "fake news" designed to "sow panic." Political editors complained about foreign coverage making Ukrainians "nervous." Even Russian Security Council Secretary Patrushev noted the irony: "Ukrainians themselves, including officials, say there is no threat."

The cruel paradox: the more Z insists on Ukrainian "subjectiveness," the harder it becomes to acknowledge the structural dependencies that actually govern Ukrainian policy options. The rhetoric enabling psychological survival prevents the strategic realism that might enable actual survival.

Shaw's solution was characteristically brutal: strip away moral theater and face the mutual culpabilities making diplomatic resolution possible. For Ukraine, this would mean distinguishing between performing agency and building it—but that would require abandoning the very discourse that makes current policy psychologically tolerable.

Political science textbooks may define subjectiveness clearly, but confusing it with sovereignty can be fatal.

## Day 1275 

*Date: 2025-08-21 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Attack of the vengeful peacemaker*

[Image]

Imagine, for a moment, the future of negotiations to end Russia’s invasion of Ukraine.

You're minding your own business, scrolling through Telegram channels during an air alert[1](#footnote-1), checking White House [briefings](https://www.whitehouse.gov/articles/2025/08/sunday-shows-president-trumps-pursuit-of-peace/) or something, and you plug a wire into the back of your head so you can physically experience the diplomatic side of bullshit.

[Image]

Now imagine surfing to the latest peace proposal. Quickly, you click on a random framework, only to find yourself drowning in "security guarantees" that guarantee nothing. You follow another thread that brings you to Marco Rubio's transformation—an experience that reminds you of the shower scene from Alfred Hitchcock's *Psycho*.

There's no escape from this theater of the absurd, where Vova is now just a misunderstood negotiating partner, and Little Marco—once Vova's harshest critic—has undergone what he calls [a "transformation" on foreign policy](https://www.nytimes.com/2025/08/20/us/politics/marco-rubio-ukraine.html). Translation: he learned to read the room, and the room belongs to Donald.

[Image]

Donald has delegated the impossible task of crafting "security guarantees" to Little Marco, who must now square the circle: reassure Ukraine while appeasing Vova, satisfy Europeans while avoiding NATO. It's diplomatic origami with a hand grenade.

With JD providing commentary from the peanut gallery.

[Video: https://youtu.be/Z0yJFK\_KVNc]

The irony is delicious. Meaghan [warns](https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/politics/trump-stands-at-a-churchill-chamberlain-crossroads-over-ukraines-sovereignty-says-kelloggs-daughter/ar-AA1KdWfn?apiversion=v2&noservercache=1&domshim=1&renderwebcomponents=1&wcseo=1&batchservertelemetry=1&noservertelemetry=1) that Donald faces a "Churchill-Chamberlain crossroads" where "you cannot trade another nation's sovereignty for the illusion of stability." Yet that's precisely what's happening, with everyone pretending otherwise.

There's something about the sense of isolation and open space in these negotiations, which evokes the Eternal Struggle—man against man against nature. Sovereignty claims all in the end, but it is the struggle over territory that defines diplomacy.

[Image]

The Kremlin continues its masterclass in gaslighting. Lavrov [suggests](https://www.reuters.com/world/europe/russia-says-talks-ukraines-security-without-moscow-are-road-nowhere-2025-08-20/) Russia wants "truly reliable" security guarantees—meaning the 2022 Istanbul framework that would neuter Ukraine permanently, ban NATO membership, and require Russian permission for Western military aid. It's like offering to guard the henhouse while sharpening your claws.

Also, there is a kick to the balls, strong enough to lift a diplomat off the ground, yet not strong enough to produce a blood-curdling shriek. It's Vova’s negotiating strategy: demand everything, concede nothing, let time do the work. He's supposedly agreed to meet Z, except Lavrov clarifies this means more working groups and preliminary discussions. It's diplomatic "check's in the mail."

Europeans keep begging for "Article 5-like" protections—NATO guarantees without NATO—while Trump rules out American troops but suggests Europeans are ready for "boots on the ground." European leaders must now sell their publics on dying for Ukrainian sovereignty while Washington washes its hands. Automatically the funniest thing about this whole circus.

The most revealing detail: analysts discuss the "gray areas" including territorial divisions, Crimea's status, and—delightfully—"Z's fate." Despite "mutual desire to remove Z," there's disagreement on methodology. Should they undermine him politically or wait for resignation-by-exhaustion?

Ukraine sits impossibly caught between Donald's impatience and Vova's patience, between European sympathy and American indifference. Z is "caught between Donald and the degradation of the front"—diplomatic speak for running out of time and options.

The proposed solution involves elaborate kabuki theater where Putin magnanimously "exchanges" Ukrainian territory he doesn't control for territory he desperately wants, while everyone pretends this constitutes negotiation. It's "yours for yours," except one side's "yours" was never theirs. Okay, it's a bit weird, but I suppose this sort of thing does happen from time to time in international relations. Whoever designed this peace process neglected to put in any actual peace.

Most remarkably, this charade sells as American diplomatic success. Rubio now soothes that "we can't end the war without talking to Vova"—conveniently ignoring whether the conversation ends the war or just pauses it until Putin wants round two.

The tragedy isn't that this might fail—it's that it might succeed, creating Meaghan's ["illusion of stability."](https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/politics/trump-stands-at-a-churchill-chamberlain-crossroads-over-ukraines-sovereignty-says-kelloggs-daughter/ar-AA1KdWfn?apiversion=v2&noservercache=1&domshim=1&renderwebcomponents=1&wcseo=1&batchservertelemetry=1&noservertelemetry=1) Europe returns to "hybrid war," Ukraine loses territory and sovereignty, Putin claims vindication.

And your pain is irrelevant.

Principles are negotiable, sovereignty is a suggestion, and peace means whatever the strongest party says it means. Churchill would be mortified; Chamberlain would feel vindicated.

# Flying Blind and Loving It

Czesław Miłosz had a useful term for people who mistake their intellectual submission for wisdom: *ketman*—the art of paying lip service to official doctrine while privately maintaining doubts. Today's Trump administration has managed something even more impressive: they've eliminated the doubts entirely by firing everyone who might have them.

The New York Times [reports](https://www.nytimes.com/2025/08/21/us/politics/trump-government-expertise.html) that Trump has gutted Russian expertise across the intelligence community. "They're flying blind without the expertise," warns one former official. But this misses the point—they *want* to fly blind. Expertise is the enemy of certainty, and certainty is Trump's brand.

Remember the old Galician Jew's wisdom: anyone claiming to be 100% right is "a fanatic, a thug, and the worst kind of rascal." Trump's approach to Putin embodies precisely this fanaticism. "I think he wants to make a deal for me," he told Macron, captured on a hot mic—the voice of a man who has already decided what reality must be.

This is *[ukrainesplaining](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/ukrainesplaining?utm_source=publication-search)*in reverse. Where the ukrainesplainers we skewered exaggerated Ukrainian agency and mystery (Snyder's breathless take on Z staying in Kyiv: "It was a mystery, in the profound sense of the word"—oh, puhleaze), Trump's team has decided Russian expertise itself is the problem. Why puzzle over Putin's actual intentions when you can simply declare yourself uniquely gifted at reading him?

Miłosz understood how this works. In *The Captive Mind*, he showed that intellectual captivity requires not just accepting official narratives, but actively rejecting inconvenient knowledge. The captive mind doesn't merely ignore expertise—it purges experts as threats to preferred reality.

"Let's just hope we aren't irradiated and freeze to death during the process"—now carries extra bite. The Biden team at least maintained the pretense of balancing competing risks while trying to avoid "direct conflict" (whatever that meant). Trump's crew has taken the cleaner approach: eliminate the risk assessors entirely.

The fired officials include a senior CIA Russia analyst, the NSA's top data scientist on Chinese cyber threats, and three people who worked on Russian influence operations. Their crime? Having institutional memory that contradicts the narrative of Trump as Putin whisperer extraordinaire.

Miłosz packed his intellectual baggage with writers who understood imperial power and its seductions. Trump's team travels lighter—they've simply discarded the baggage handlers who might point out inconvenient destinations.

## Day 1279 

*Date: 2025-08-25 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Occidental or oriental? *

[Image]

Russia’s giveaways:

> "I didn’t say they conceded on everything, but what they have conceded is the recognition that Ukraine will have territorial integrity after the war; they've recognised that they're not going to be able to install a puppet regime in Kyiv – that was of course a major demand at the beginning – and importantly, they've acknowledged that there is going to be some security guarantee." - JD ([Meet the Press](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YRwZE5MJ5co), August 24, 2025)

[Image]

Sigh.

*The Occidental[1](#footnote-1)* (*Мирні переговори*), formerly called *Переможемо разом* (*We Will Win Together*) and *Кримська платформа* (*Crimea Platform*), is the newly reopened diplomatic restaurant at the Willard Hotel on Pennsylvania Avenue. On Thursday nights before the midterm elections, they used to have Ukrainian folk music and Z would show up, fresh from awarding "National Legends" to opera singers and high-jumpers while his missiles sat unused in Pentagon-approved storage. It was a pretty cramped place, and it began to occur to defense officials, "Should we approve ATACMS strikes this Thursday or not?" They realized that regulars—mostly corrupt arms dealers, think tank fellows and money managers—would be asking themselves the same question.

The problem is not new. In physics, there's the spherical cow assumption: "Consider a spherical cow of uniform density." It's a joke about how theorists oversimplify complex problems to make them mathematically tractable. Political upheaval—like a war—just adds another layer of comedy.

The more things change, they stay the same. Today JD Vance meets you at the entrance to the negotiations, where little ATACMS missiles hang down from the ceiling, blocked by Pentagon approval mechanisms. This is where Belarusian bitumen wholesalers rub shoulders with members of Ukraine's defense ministry, investment bankers and peace process architects from Washington's impoverished think tanks. Photographs of magical territorial concessions are posted daily by analysts on Twitter. If you're lucky, Mykola the Pork Chop might be there, along with Elbridge Colby practicing his color-coded weapons taxonomy, while Z livestreams from Kyiv about "Ukrainian legends" and how "everything will be alright" and "we will embrace each other, so gently."

The spherical cow approach to ending-the-war negotiations assumes perfect information, rational actors, and frictionless diplomatic surfaces. In reality, Z can't fire his missiles without Pentagon approval while Trump tweets about the impossibility of winning without offense. Putin processes his abandonment issues in an empty chair that may or may not represent genuine territorial integrity commitments. The Donbas sits at the center like Schrödinger's province—simultaneously Ukrainian and Russian until someone observes the final settlement.

Vance's concessions revelation is classic spherical cow thinking: assume Russia recognizes Ukrainian territorial integrity, assume they've abandoned regime change, assume meaningful security guarantees. But spherical cows don't lactate, and spherical peace processes don't end wars. The real negotiations happen between Thursday night regulars who know that if everyone uses the same pure strategy—territorial compromise, security guarantees, frozen conflicts—it's guaranteed to fail, especially when one side is awarding medals to opera singers while the other side systematically destroys power grids.

Most predictable outcome? More theoretical frameworks, more spherical cow assumptions about rational actors and clean territorial divisions. The restaurant stays open, the geishas keep greeting diplomats at the door, and somewhere in the back room, people who actually understand the physics of war continue to block the weapons that might make the mathematics work.

[1](#footnote-anchor-1)

[Occidental,](https://www.google.com/search?q=occidental+definition&oq=occidental+definition&gs_lcrp=EgZjaHJvbWUyDggAEEUYORhGGPkBGIAEMgcIARAAGIAEMggIAhAAGBYYHjIICAMQABgWGB4yCAgEEAAYFhgeMggIBRAAGBYYHjIICAYQABgWGB4yCAgHEAAYFhgeMggICBAAGBYYHjIICAkQABgWGB7SAQg0NjQ3ajBqNKgCALACAA&sourceid=chrome&ie=UTF-8) *def* — of or relating to the West

## Day 1280 

*Date: 2025-08-26 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Reliably unreliable *

[Image]

It is unsettling at best to see someone once considered a hero descend into madness, yet curiously satisfying to see the product of this madness cleanly and elegantly blown to smithereens.

Yesterday I promised myself I wouldn't write about Ukraine this week. That lasted exactly 24 hours. But the connections between yesterday's post about military careerists and war profiteers and what I'm seeing in today's Ukraine discourse are too obvious to ignore[1](#footnote-1).

I had great hopes for becoming a reliable source of comment and information, freed from the sentimental histrionics and pre-emptive ridicule of opposing views that characterize war bloggers, who have evolved predictably: first licking their wounds after arguing all-out invasion was unlikely, then taking grim pleasure in cataloguing every mistake made by those resisting occupation, and finally coalescing—with shuddering inevitability—into a lockstep neoauthoritarian echo chamber. [The most cynical observations we made last year](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-1013) about those who would exploit inconceivable human tragedy for political gain have not only proven true, but have somehow become immune to criticism.

[Yekaterina](https://www.instagram.com/p/C59Esv4tahI/) waking up a last year.

Conversely, I expect to be free of the gloomy handwringing that characterizes some in opposition, among whom, now and again, a flurry of concern arises over What Must Be Done. It shouldn't be in the least surprising that xenophobic idiots driven by bloodlust and malformed MAGAism are sharing their opinions about how Ukraine should surrender every chance they get.

What will be made evident, I hope, is that annexation of any part of Ukraine is not a reflexive ideological matter, born out of party allegiances, 'anti-Americanism,' or what colour underwear one prefers. It is rather that the world is being sold a bill of goods: one that is apparently having some success convincing people the invasion has anything to do with the restoration of Russia's empire, or revenge for the implosion of the USSR, or weapons of mass destruction, or the protection of security interests, or—most gallingly of all—'regime change' for the good of the fraternal relations of Slavic brothers.

As opposed, let's say, to it being about salvaging at any cost power grabs that have been, from every imaginable perspective, unmitigated disasters.

It's amusing to note that the most lurid Cold War fantasies of covert propaganda spread on behalf of our own President's Office by chatbots have been rendered irrelevant by a chattering horde of useful idiots [now serving it up for free](https://x.com/Sassovivente/status/1957804491991839053) every day: who needs black ops when you've got an account on X.

[Image]

Speaking of useful idiots, yesterday's exploration of how military experience gets packaged and sold brought me back to those two big breaks on the stroll downtown that happened in between Meaghan [luxuriating on Potemkin’s steps](https://www.instagram.com/p/DNsp1A83Mx0/), standing [in a field of ripe sunflowers](https://www.instagram.com/p/DNYd7gGi60r/) or keening [at GUR's memorial on Rybalsky Island](https://www.instagram.com/p/DNffTNSubXJ/?img_index=1).

Raise your hand if you enlisted in the US military because of the 2003 invasion of Iraq.

[Video: https://youtu.be/fKGc\_xaQkUU]

Which brings me directly to John, again, who today [opines](https://www.washingtonexaminer.com/opinion/beltway-confidential/3780298/trump-utterly-incoherent-ukraine-strategy-john-bolton/) that Donald's interference in our tragic bloody war has been incoherent.

[Image]

*Incoherent?* More like psychotic.

And back to [award-winning Keith](https://x.com/ZUa/status/1960023126336500058), who, we recall, re-raised his hand in 2003 and played a critical role in the disastrous US occupation of Iraq as the director of operations of the Coalition Provisional Authority.

That didn't end well.

The pattern here isn't subtle: the same voices that cheered us into Iraq are now positioning themselves as Ukraine experts, their previous disasters somehow qualifying them as strategic geniuses rather than disqualifying them entirely. It's a remarkable feat of rehabilitation—one that would be impressive if it weren't so predictably, er, unhelpful.

But hey, at least they're consistent in their inconsistency.

## Day 1281 

*Date: 2025-08-27 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Performative narcissism *

Fidel’s government meetings were typically more formal affairs focused on implementing policy in a one-party state, whereas Donald’s televised orgies more resemble reality television, with cabinet members competing to offer the best boot licks.

[Image]

The key difference is that Fidel, whatever his faults, didn't need to stage ego-feeding sessions where officials took turns flattering him for hours. Performative narcissism isn’t uniquely Trump’s innovation in modern governance. Lukashenko has been practicing it for three decades.

---

Donald mentioned Ukraine [13 times](https://rollcall.com/factbase/trump/transcript/donald-trump-remarks-cabinet-meeting-white-house-august-26-2025/) during his marathon cabinet meeting on Thursday[1](#footnote-1). He didn't say anything new.

Since his return to the White House, Team USA has imposed **zero penalties** on Russia for its genocidal war against Ukraine, now in its 12th year. Instead, Donald has interrupted arms deliveries, cancelled assistance programs, ended international isolation of Putin and repeated Kremlin talking points.

The reason Team USA doesn't have an ambassador to Ukraine? The old one resigned rather than participate in appeasing the aggressor.

In Donald's fluid understanding of military ratios, Russia keeps growing. On August 26, he claimed Ukraine was fighting someone "[15 times your size.](https://rollcall.com/factbase/trump/transcript/donald-trump-remarks-cabinet-meeting-white-house-august-26-2025/)" Just six days earlier on Fox & Friends, Russia was only "[10 times your size.](https://ca.news.yahoo.com/trump-blames-ukraine-taking-nation-132221123.html)" Apparently Russia gained 50% more mass in less than a week. At this rate, Russia will be roughly the size of Jupiter by Christmas.

The get-together was pure performance art. As the New York Times observed[2](#footnote-2), it resembled reality television more than governance, with Donald demanding praise while cameras rolled. Ukraine was just one item on his "ever-growing list" of pet peeves—sandwiched between complaints about windmills, traffic medians, and water pressure.

The star performer was Steve Witkoff, Donald's "peace envoy" and golf buddy, who gushed that Donald deserved the Nobel Peace Prize as "the single finest candidate since the Nobel Peace...this Noble Award was ever talked about." **Peace through strength really, really works**, Witkoff declared

[Image]

.

This is the same biscuit who, months earlier, [demonstrated](https://abdymok.substack.com/p/day-1123?utm_source=publication-search) his profound expertise on Tucker Carlson. He couldn't name all four Ukrainian regions Russia annexed, stumbling through "Donbas, Crimea... uh... Luhansk... uh... and there are two others." He falsely claimed Ukrainian forces were "surrounded" in Kursk when intelligence showed they weren't. Most impressively, he parroted Putin's propaganda about sham "referendums" showing residents wanted Russian rule—when no legitimate self-determination has ever occurred in any part of Ukraine.

But don't worry—Witkoff has repeatedly assured Donald he's "the only person who could solve the Russia-Ukraine war." Donald added with characteristic false modesty: "I have no ego when it comes to this stuff."

**Peace through stupidity really, really does not work.** Team USA's appeasement strategy has only made the war longer and more deadly. Case in point: immediately after Donald got off the phone with Putin recently, swarms of Iranian Shahed drones descended on Kyiv, followed by North Korean ballistic missiles. Because nothing says "peace through strength" like your negotiating partner bombing your ally while you're supposedly negotiating.

As I gazed across the Dnipro after that morning's bombardment, the disconnect was crystal clear: Donald is obvi Putin's chump.

To quote a former heat-seeking douche rocket for Donald’s ass:

> If America presides over negotiations that result in a win for Putin, we won’t be ending this war — we’ll be guaranteeing the next one[3](#footnote-3) - Former US Secretary of State Mike Pompeo

But why listen to Mike when you have a real estate mogul who can't locate Zaporizhia on a map negotiating the fate of 44 million Ukrainians?[4](#footnote-4)

Unfortunately, surrender isn't an option—that would mean death or exile. So here we are: a Nobel Peace Prize nominee whose "peace through strength" consists of mathematical confusion, geographical ignorance, and immediate post-negotiation bombings. But at least the Nobel committee knows where to send the participation trophy.

Every miliblogger with a Wi-Fi connection keeps their feeds obsessively refreshed during air raids – because nothing says "war coverage" like performative doom-scrolling.

These three handles can split any room of thinking humans: half snorting with derision, the other half practicing the glazed-eye worship perfected by Z-cultists and MAGA maniacs.

Masterpieces of modern naval-gazing – impeccably formatted, stubbornly uncompromising, each a virtuoso performance of saying absolutely nothing. X's core ideological mission (free speech as digital town square, etc.) echoes through every glowing example of this crap.

[Image]

Each is a virtuoso performance of modern social networking, impeccably concise and laid out, stubbornly free of compromise and concessions. They are strong and well presented, with X’s central, ongoing (and, here, vastly reduced) politically charged *emploi* – that info deserves unencumbered presentation – ringing out in example after glowing example.

Anguish is more illuminating than the Home. Freedom. Liberty. Victory. stuff, which is, er, weird. War's pressure and speed should kill the "Tweet-think-Tweet-rethink" cycle, yet somehow it keeps creating vast canyons for the BOGSAT gravitational pull that defines X.

Lovely to scroll through while, oh, waiting for the steamer to adjust from espresso to milk setting, or the next cruise missile to whistle by overhead.

In other news, here’s a snappier version of practicing philosopher Volodymyr’s [Telegram post](https://t.me/v_pastukhov/1634) yesterday.

> **The Chemistry Problem**
>
> Trump and Putin developed solid chemistry in Alaska, but chemistry without action is just talk. Putin's demand for "voluntary surrender" turns their supposed friendship into something that stinks.
>
> Do they both get this? Absolutely. That's why there was no dinner—they lit the fire but never cooked the meal. Yet both keep playing at friendship, hoping to solve their problem at Ukraine's expense. Neither can afford to drop the act yet.
>
> Trump pauses, letting Putin "prove himself" by taking what he wants by force. Putin pauses, letting Trump pressure Z behind the scenes.
>
> **The Berserker Factor**
>
> Z looks like the main obstacle to their deal, but this misses the point. Z's will today is Ukraine's will—however controversial that sounds.
>
> This reminds me of a childhood book about berserkers—Vikings who fought in a frenzy, feeling no pain. That's Ukrainian society now. Not every individual Ukrainian, obviously, but as a collective political force: a berserker, full of rage. In this state, voluntary compromise is impossible.
>
> This is what everyone misses when discussing war or peace. Rational analysis fails because Ukrainian society isn't operating rationally and likely can't.
>
> When Trump tells Ukraine it has "bad cards," he doesn't grasp that Ukraine stopped looking at cards long ago. Americans base predictions on the possible—politics as the art of the possible. Ukrainians practice politics based on the impossible. In Ukraine today, expecting miracles is the basis for political decisions.
>
> **The Gun Problem**
>
> Trump and Putin understand each other perfectly. Their problem is that Ukrainians will only accept compromise under one condition: after the front has already collapsed catastrophically. The mere *threat* of collapse doesn't work when society expects miracles—no threat feels real.
>
> Peace won't come from Trump's "kind words" but from whether he'll put a gun to those words—whether he'll risk the ugly ultimatum of cutting off all Ukrainian support. Without that gun, Ukraine won't accept the deal. If Z suddenly changes course, I'll know the gun found his temple.

I disagree with a couple of points and will elaborate later.

> The 9M727 (or 9M728) cruise missile used by the Russian Iskander-K system is described as having a CEP of around 7-10 meters, making it highly accurate for striking tactical and [operational targets](https://www.google.com/search?sca_esv=93b66263c54a1938&cs=0&sxsrf=AE3TifPznCO_XwgMpPREVMJ46IUGN3S2HQ%3A1756368644688&q=operational+targets&sa=X&ved=2ahUKEwiT64aJh62PAxVLSfEDHbM0HPYQxccNegQIAxAB&mstk=AUtExfCXo211psAxDCJT-3MGso6OMCwpSd6AprFK7PtX89vEFMYcSDzQXCtdXgjRdislVla3l0X1rU707SsJZj_7gn1DibuPooJeZ6nKg5cBMZV2JS2DYXeZlafjRlT2h4cNuEbu2vgaqroOh9iUHIGy2ioan7P-iEeEuxfdPY0mnd5GVVYv7SPcSfcXZjoVAitNX_JZRvsqztWbS5q3GTj5coiRzcN-xKm8r7YTw6_wq9h2HknfP-uODzTjOwkdOhjwU0D9rV8oW7xI3W2v6sKlcM_gEQx7HvFu2fC5hM1fQAe7DA&csui=3) like command posts, supply depots, and communication nodes. Its accuracy is enabled by advanced guidance, which can be further enhanced by intelligence, surveillance, and reconnaissance (ISR) assets such as UAVs to refine targeting data. - [Google AI](https://www.google.com/search?q=9M727+Iskander-K+accuracy&sca_esv=93b66263c54a1938&sxsrf=AE3TifPKrDmTraLuoxkM3GkLA-iXtfOSew%3A1756368621138&ei=7Q6waJGbCKDYwPAPprKd8AU&ved=2ahUKEwiT64aJh62PAxVLSfEDHbM0HPYQ0NsOegQILhAA&uact=5&sclient=gws-wiz-serp&udm=50&fbs=AIIjpHxU7SXXniUZfeShr2fp4giZ1Y6MJ25_tmWITc7uy4KIetBXe9Do9DAuamxeB-puw_YsNKwkQmpdBnXmV_Wcu-Aa6tSphtaa0Ii6-dV_VFDbcaQwygNIqWUHPcnbM0-SOPe6qDBc4lbleFM0Ef8p5vSiaDvKzgyK3d7UUzE1jet64Avu0bGTMGc3rSGg4s3kA_EtbsoTRqxqT2GQb5ufMw1rPr3C5w&aep=10&ntc=1&mtid=Qw-waJPnCdasxc8PiI79kA4&mstk=AUtExfDhrL5bFpCoLnyCsSZwfaL9o3tka-_rKSOuOPrA_41i5IJsP9wUuGNoeqVwgUDiN_FtWSw4bgYUEOdXp-e_cpGdhf66zPRKPD3lOYVUK2tuypkB0ZAjDSSyZgawHHauop0ICpDnrhBJUJQUSAfjZeQrAaKCOqYioPa3sXC0Vi6Z8wf06EiJxpk1QcdEvAqKCzdMQfEWK9QIAOM7518XN80WlsZplhTrZRaX_9GLuUuVZXbheJY8jN_H-IV7VeTKpL8cPOxA10deUS8MCJZ4qKjOKEO3DRVj5OUJSR6IMTxFvMoj_gBD-ktqy5i46Ut-0rM2DdinYyFOUw&csuir=1)

So, we can’t rule out that Russia has entered the precision-strike phase of its genocidal war. For now, we’ll go with the simpler explanation.

**Occam's razor analysis:**

The simpler explanation requires fewer assumptions:

- Russia launched missiles at a central Kyiv location
- The area happened to contain media and international offices (along with many other buildings)
- Missiles hit their intended coordinates with high precision

The more complex explanation would require additional assumptions:

- Russia specifically researched and mapped media/international office locations
- Russia made a strategic decision to target these specific types of organizations
- Russia chose this particular moment to escalate to "precision-strike phase of genocidal war"
- The targeting was intentionally symbolic rather than tactically motivated

The death count has risen to more than two dozen and Mayor Klitschko has declared August 29 a day of mourning.

## Day 1283 

*Date: 2025-08-29 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Waffle town*

[Image]

*A cloudless late summer morning in Kyiv.*

Peeked at what Team Russia blew up yesterday morning on the right bank of the Dnipro. Not pretty.

Two 9M727 Iskander-K cruise missiles decided to redecorate most of a city block, blowing out windows like some deranged urban renewal project. Didn't have time to hunt for craters—too busy dodging glass. Across the river in Darnytsky district, missiles turned a 5-story residential building into rubble. Police bodycam footage captured the first responders stumbling through that particular horror show. Libkos was there, too.

[Image]

Meanwhile, somewhere in the rarefied air of international diplomacy, European officials are having what can only be described as an existential crisis. They're still "mystified," apparently, by what exactly Putin promised Trump's envoy Steve Witkoff during their cozy August chat in Moscow.

Here's the thing about Putin's promises: they tend to explode on contact with reality.

While diplomats parsed the fine print of "de jure recognition" and territorial concessions, Putin was busy demonstrating his negotiating style with high explosives. The Trump administration rolled out an actual red carpet for Vlad in Alaska—complete with B-2 flyover and presidential applause—while Kyiv residents got a different kind of morning show: incoming cruise missiles.

Team USA's **pathetic attempts** at both-sidesing this conflict is almost performance art at this point. At the White House briefing, they managed to equate Ukraine hitting military targets with Russia vaporizing apartment buildings. It's like comparing a surgeon's scalpel to a chainsaw and calling them both "cutting tools."

> Q: Two questions on two separate issues. First of all, the drone and missile strikes that took place overnight in Ukraine, General Keith Kellogg, the president's special envoy, has described these attacks as egregious. Do you agree with that description? And do you view President Putin as an impediment to getting peace between these two countries?
>
> A: Well, it's not about if I agree, it's about what the president thinks and I know what the president thinks because I talked to him about this. He was not happy about this news, but he was also not surprised. These are two countries that have been at war for a very long time. Russia launched this attack on Kyiv.
>
> And likewise, Ukraine recently dealt a blow to Russia's oil refineries. They have taken out, as a matter of fact, 20 percent of Russia's oil refinery capacity over the course of their attacks throughout the month of August. So the president is continuing to watch this intently and this killing, unfortunately, will continue as long as the war continues, which is why the president wants it to end.
>
> And that's why he's worked harder than anyone to end this war, which would have never started if he were president. But perhaps, both sides of this war are not ready to end it themselves. The president wants it to end, but the leaders of these two countries need it to end and must want it to end as well.
>
> And I think the president will make some additional statements on this later. ([RollCall](https://rollcall.com/factbase/trump/transcript/donald-trump-press-conference-briefing-karoline-leavitt-august-28-2025/), August 28, White House briefing)

The diplomatic dance continues with all the grace of a three-legged waltz. Trump's peace envoy—a real estate guy with zero diplomatic experience—apparently thought he'd cracked the code when Putin offered to give up "legal claims" to territories while conveniently forgetting to mention the thousands of Russian soldiers who'd be staying put. It's like agreeing to sell your house but keeping the keys and half your furniture.

European officials warned their American counterparts that Putin's word games usually end with more Ukrainian cities looking like today's crime scene. But hey, who needs details when you're making history?

Trump promised to end this war in 24 hours 53 times on the campaign trail. We're seven months in, and his latest diplomatic breakthrough coincided perfectly with this morning's missile barrage. 23 dead, including children—but at least we're "watching this intently."

## Day 1287

*Date: 2025-09-02 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Recht präzise*

[Image]

Listen to Ursula lugubriously lip up Europe's latest black eye in the [Der Spiegel](https://www.spiegel.de/ausland/ukraine-ursula-von-der-leyen-spricht-von-recht-praezisen-plaenen-fuer-eu-truppen-a-83a04cea-9679-4f67-b3b8-66bd3c7f6147) - "quite precise plans" (as opposed to "pretty precise" - [FT](https://www.ft.com/content/8ade14ca-7aa1-4413-887b-59712037665c)) for a multinational troop deployment to Ukraine, she says, with all the conviction of someone reading from a teleprompter while the building burns down behind her. The gist of the bit? A couple of wooly-headed Eurocrats responding to Donald's latest fit of foreign policy lunacy, dressed up as post-conflict security guarantees. That's it. Sustainable outcomes are now impossible. Typical prefab stroke material.

But Ursula takes it so seriously that she forgets there's ridicule already dripping from the confab (and that everyone in Washington's dank Trumpian circle-jerk has already [ranted about it](https://www.cnas.org/publications/podcast/ukraine-negotiations-confusing-activity-with-progress)) and puts on the tired old threadbare diplomatic gloves to dance like an idiot. "President Trump reassured us that there will be [an] American presence as part of the backstop," she bleats, as if Trump's word has ever been worth the hot air it takes to produce it. Might as well have simply stopped after saying 'Let's assume the president can put himself on the back burner for a while…'

Meanwhile, reality intrudes with its usual rudeness. Belaya Tserkva in Kyiv region was [hammered](https://www.pravda.com.ua/news/2025/09/2/7528890/) last night - because while Europe draws up its pretty precise plans, Putin draws blood. Russian drones knocked out power to 60,000 Ukrainians overnight, hitting Odesa and Chernihiv regions like clockwork. The Dragon keeps feeding while the knights debate seating arrangements at the round table.

---

[Image]

---

The theater reaches peak absurdity when we learn that Vladimir and Xi are having [their own beano](https://www.reuters.com/world/china/after-talks-with-xi-modi-putin-says-nato-enlargement-has-be-addressed-ukraine-2025-09-01/) on the sidelines of some showpiece summit in China. Putin's sticking around for Wednesday's military parade - 80 years since WWII ended, and here we are again, watching Europe plan another intervention while dictators toast their partnership. "The longer the war drags on, the more Russia will need China's assistance," notes one analyst, as if this weren't blindingly obvious to anyone who's been awake since 2022.

Newly elected right-wing Polish President Karol Nawrocki [visits the White House tomorrow](https://bnn-news.com/polands-foreign-policy-from-regional-leader-to-setting-star-271454). Lots can go wrong. Nawrocki recently demanded, stupidly, reparations from Germany for World War II. Apparently, the war didn't start in 1939 and Russia owes nothing. This is the quality of leadership Europe's betting its "pretty precise plans" on - a man who thinks grievance theater from 1945 is a winning diplomatic strategy in 2025.

The Kremlin, naturally, claims that European powers are hindering Trump's peace efforts. Dmitry Peskov calls it the "European party of war" - which is rich, coming from the people who just spent another night dropping drones on Ukrainian power plants. "We are ready to resolve the problem by political and diplomatic means," says the spokesman for the regime that's been methodically destroying civilian infrastructure for three years running. "But so far we do not see reciprocity from Kyiv in this."

Reciprocity. As if Ukraine's failure to surrender fast enough is the real obstacle to peace.

And through it all, Z huddles with German lawmakers and vows retaliation with "[new deep strikes](https://t.me/V_Zelenskiy_official/15891)" planned inside Russia, while German Chancellor Friedrich Merz solemnly intones that the war could last "a long time" and won't end "at Ukraine's expense." Everyone positioning, everyone posturing, everyone pretending their latest statement matters more than the last.

The Dragon feeds. The knights plan. The maiden bleeds.

## Day 1288 

*Date: 2025-09-03 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Missiles, visas, village *

“I don’t relish the idea of taking life, but I’m doing what I feel is right,” says Derek Huffman, formerly an Arizona welder, now a Russian conscript[1](#footnote-1). What he’s “doing” is helping reduce Ukraine’s power grid to rubble while his wife livestreams their “escape from LGBT indoctrination.” The reward: expedited Russian citizenship and a [YouTube following](https://www.youtube.com/@HuffmanTime). The extraordinary life he advertises is complicity in war crimes, branded and monetized.

Two weeks ago, Putin and Trump convened in Alaska to “end the war.” It produced exactly what one would expect from three hours of cameras and handshakes: nothing. Since then, Moscow has treated Ukraine to another missile deluge while Xi, Putin, and a gout-ridden Lukashenko waddled across Beijing red carpets. Xi spoke of a “more just international order.” Translation: fuck the current one. Lukashenko, barely able to shuffle a hundred feet, has been demoted from Europe’s last dictator to geopolitical slapstick.

The contrast is instructive. The West confuses diplomacy with stagecraft—[Macron with his summits](https://www.lemonde.fr/en/international/article/2025/09/01/macron-and-starmer-will-co-chair-paris-summit-on-ukraine-security-guarantees-with-Z_6744932_4.html), Starmer with his [“security guarantees.](https://www.bbc.com/news/articles/cx2qr08l1yko)” Russia answers with missile diplomacy and the strategic flick of the thermostat. CNN, in its infinite solemnity, warns that “[winter is coming](https://edition.cnn.com/2025/09/02/politics/winter-is-coming-for-ukraine-what-it-will-take-to-keep-the-lights-on),” as though blackouts were meteorology rather than deliberate war crimes measured in degrees Celsius.

[Image]

Meanwhile, Ukraine produces lifestyle content. [The Kyiv Independent](http://kyivindependent.com)’s new pitch: “[Dare to Ukraine Village](https://kyivindependent.com/dare-to-ukraine/),” in which a host abandons Kyiv for a $500 rural budget and a dog named Letti. Raw, real, occasionally ridiculous—so long as the power stays on. Chess nights in Kyiv, erotic calendars, influencer vlogs: the show goes on, commodified survival with sponsors attached.

[Image]

Moscow, too, has joined the content economy. Huffman is the poster child: an American who fled pronouns only to embrace genocide. Sholem Aleichem might have said: you cannot spit out your citizenship. But you can apparently exchange it for complicity, provided you upload faithfully.

The grotesque does not end there. After MP Andriy Parubiy’s confessed killer made headlines, his ex-wife took to Facebook to reclaim her son’s memory: divorced since 1998, she raised him alone, watched him die as a junior sergeant with the 93rd Brigade in Bakhmut. “My son, a hero, died for Ukraine. Heroes do not die!” she wrote[2](#footnote-2). While parents bury their sons, Western readers are invited to marvel at budget survival blogs.

And still the clicks accumulate. Martyrdom-by-choice in Moscow, Kyiv’s lifestyle experiments, CNN’s seasonal dispatches. The algorithm is impartial to tragedy. It rewards the packaged story—whether genocide, stupidity or death.

The fiddler plays while the roof burns. Xi smirks, Lukashenko limps, Putin plots, and the West applauds its own handshakes. Winter approaches, armed and deliberate. And somewhere, inevitably, someone will turn it into content—raw, real, occasionally ridiculous—streaming live, assuming the lights stay on.

[Image]

[1](#footnote-anchor-1)

[The Americans moving to wartime Russia for ‘traditional values.’](https://archive.ph/wjiY4#selection-1885.0-1891.73) *Moscow promotes visa scheme aimed at those disaffected by western society* ([The Financial Times,](http://ft.com) September 2, 2025)

[2](#footnote-anchor-2)

> I want to clarify the situation. There is a flurry of information on the Internet. True and absurd. I broke up with my ex-husband in 1998. And officially with a document - in 2000. I raised my son alone. Sometimes the father would show up. Like on September 1st in the first grade or for some significant event. He communicated with his son, but very rarely. When Lemberg went to war, they had a big fight. And Lemberg blocked his ancestor everywhere. Because one was a patriot, and the other was not. After my son's death, I had to communicate with his father. Documents, investigators, DNA, etc. There was neither friendship nor enmity. Our infrequent communication was only about the disappearance of my son. I raised Mikhail-Victor to be a real courageous man. You can read about this in my book, where there is no mention of his father.
>
> The news from the court in the case of the murder of Andriy Parubiy shocked our family. It is important that the memory of Mykhailo is not distorted in this situation.
>
> My son, a hero, died for Ukraine. He went through Kyiv, Zaporizhia, Mykolaiv and himself asked for a transfer to Bakhmut to the 93rd brigade. To the hottest point. He was a junior sergeant, commander of a reconnaissance platoon. And he died like a Hero. Heroes do not die!

Woland is a literary character from Mikhail Bulgakov's novel The Master and Margarita. He is one of the names of the devil in the work, a mysterious and powerful professor of black magic who arrives in Soviet Moscow in the 1930s with his retinue.

The remark about the suddenness of death turns out to be prophetic - soon after these words, Berlioz does indeed drop dead unexpectedly and absurdly. More exactly, slips on sunflower oil on the trolley bus track and get decapitated.

In the context of discussions about prolonging life, Bulgakov’s Woland would probably be quite ironic about human attempts to cheat death.

Our thoughts this morning were with Trey, standing bug-eyed on the balcony of the Intercontinental Hotel in Kyiv, blue helmet in hand, waiting…

*[@TreyYingst 6h: Air defense active on the outskirts of Kyiv.](https://x.com/TreyYingst/status/1965529767035457762)*  
*[@TreyYingst 7h: Air raids sirens sounding across Kyiv.](https://x.com/TreyYingst/status/1965512799209357535)*

Putin has put Trump in a very difficult position. All those drones — some launched from Belarus — flying into Poland. If this is not a deliberate provocation by Putin aimed at humiliating NATO, then it is a spectacular fiasco engineered by the recently decorated Chief of Russia's General Staff Valery Gerasimov. You decide which is more annoying.

Occam's Razor suggests the simpler explanation: this wasn't grand strategy but the predictable consequence of launching hundreds of missiles and drones in the general direction of targets near NATO's border. When you fire that much ordnance with Soviet-era accuracy systems, some of it will inevitably go astray. The question isn't whether Putin intended this specific outcome, but whether he cared enough about the consequences to prevent it.

When you systematically dismantle the rules that constrain behavior, you shouldn't be surprised when the chaos spreads in directions you didn't anticipate.

The latest air attack comes just hours after rioters burned down the parliament building in Kathmandu, Israel bombed Doha, and glide bombs wiped out 24 residents of Yarova collecting their pensions. Coincidence? Perhaps. Or perhaps we're witnessing what happens when the post-war international order — those boring, liberal constraints on state behavior — continues to crumble.

As Nikolayevsky Vanek [noted](https://t.me/vanek_nikolaev/35811) on Telegram this morning:

> Dear whoever just woke up,  
> You just slept through another combined attack. The main strike was on Zhytomyr, but they also hit Vinnytsia and Lviv. The attack, like the previous one, was very vague. A peculiarity: more than 10 Shahed UAVs left Ukraine's air space and flew into Poland.  
> **This has never happened before.**  
> I am sure that the Poles will record fewer. I would not expect any particular reaction.

[Image]

But the Poles are now very much part of our House Dance. Defense Minister Władysław Kosiniak-Kamysz is cutting short his London meetings to rush home. President Karol Nawrocki is convening emergency security sessions. They've shortened military reporting times to six hours in the eastern provinces — those red zones [on the map](https://x.com/terytorialsi/status/1965616716177641629) where Russian debris now litters Polish soil.

*ALERT GOTOWOŚCI WOT W związku z naruszeniem polskiej przestrzeni powietrznej...*

[Image]

The Polish alert reads like a bureaucrat's fever dream: "Please remain calm while we figure out whether these are Russian missile fragments or just really aggressive litter."

What's darkly comic is how Putin's "just do things" philosophy creates problems he never intended. Those drones over Poland weren't testing NATO's resolve — they were simply the price of doing missile business without quality control. When you abandon all constraints, your chaos becomes everyone else's emergency meeting.

The Poles are now learning what Ukrainians have known for years: sovereignty is a subscription service, and Putin just canceled everyone's plan.

It's all anti-climactic, until one explodes in your living room. Or your neighbor's.

## Day 1296

*Date: 2025-09-11 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*NATO's funeral pyre*

[Image]

**What happened:** There were drones over Poland yesterday. Some time ago, NATO promised Article 5 would guarantee Poland's safety. The smirking chimp in the Kremlin sent the drones anyway. The alliance, inside its Brussels headquarters, was very excited to issue a strongly-worded statement.

Ukraine's former Commander-in-Chief Valeriy Zaluzhny [has for months](https://eutoday.net/zaluzhny-nato-is-not-ready-for-the-war/) been warning that NATO might "go the way of the United Nations—paralysed by its own structure and outdated assumptions."

But Western officials, including US Ambassador to NATO Matthew Whitaker, keep insisting the alliance has never been stronger.

[Video: https://youtu.be/voNwoRuCW5g]

These are the inevitable posturings from those who daily recite Cold War catechisms via PowerPoint, from those who glibly claim readiness for wars they fundamentally misunderstand, from those so fully infused with bureaucratic self-preservation that it no longer matters what the threat is.

Someone just asked what I thought of this.

Zaluzhny was right, obviously.

Apropos of Putin's message to NATO:

> "Let your allies walk past my drones without air defense, their Article 5 guarantees left at headquarters, while you stay three time zones away from any real response. I pledge complete safety."

Which brings me to the recipe for institutional collapse:

Take one military alliance, aged 75 years. Stuff it with procedures designed for fighting the last war. Season with strategic complacency and American indifference. Should the alliance not be prepared for modern warfare, pretend it is anyway. If you can't be bothered updating doctrine for drone swarms, issue another communiqué and let's get on with it.

Roast slowly until the contradictions are exposed and the whole thing falls apart. Serves nobody.

The drones over Poland are a pop quiz NATO hasn't studied for. While Zaluzhny explained that modern warfare runs on "drones, algorithms, and high-precision systems," NATO was shopping for expensive ways to intercept $50,000 drones with million-dollar missiles. It's bringing cavalry to cyber-war.

Putin chose his moment exquisitely—Trump back in office, European allies thoroughly debased by their diplomatic genuflections, the transatlantic façade worn thin. Those drones didn't just violate Polish airspace; they violated the premise that NATO's enemies still play by NATO's rules.

Zaluzhny warned this war would "last as long as it takes for a new technological equilibrium to emerge." Putin is building that equilibrium while NATO tries to remember where they put the old one.

The alliance will now discover whether Article 5 is a security guarantee or just expensive letterhead.

My money's on letterhead.

After the Russian invasion of Ukraine in February 2022, Kirk characterized the Russo-Ukrainian War as a "border dispute" and spread false claims from Russian state media that Ukrainian forces were firing mortar shells at a Russian separatist enclave in Ukraine[1](#footnote-1).

Additionally, Kirk expressed skepticism about U.S. military aid to Ukraine. "I don't love the idea of sending arms to Ukraine[2](#footnote-2)," he said.

[Video: https://youtu.be/w3-bOlqL0mM]

Kirk’s endorsement of Russia's unlawful Crimean occupation and blaming US officials for creating hostility with Putin and amplification of hairbrained theories about why media coverage of Iryna Zarutska's murder is being minimized reveal a consistent approach of advancing controversial positions regardless of their basis in reality.

We're all familiar with the cosmological anthropic principle—the woo-ish argument made by some scientists turned amateur philosophers that if not God, then *something* must have fine-tuned the fundamental constants of the universe to allow for life.

As a professional expert on Russia, Belarus, and Ukraine, I've noticed something troubling among some *influencers* who venture into my field. These people seem to believe that different rules about facts and debate apply when they become amateur experts about our bloody little war.

Stay-behind saboteur in Belarus during World War II Valentyn Taras in the day captured this perfectly when asked about the resistance movement—he described academic speculation abroad about stay-behind saboteurs in Belarus as "playing historical tennis without a net."

[Video: https://youtu.be/sgfy9lans1s]

- Claims fly everywhere
- No rules to determine what's \*in\* or \*out\*
- Historical \*facts\* scatter wildly
- No way to score legitimate points

It's time we put some nets back up.
