- Framework assurances on security for Ukraine (3 points),
- Commitments of the United States of America regarding NATO (4 points),
- Agreement between Russia and the United States of America (12 points).

27+12=39. That’s a lot of points. It’s a big mess.

---

Z and his top managers are indeed implicated in backoffice kickback schemes involving state-owned enterprises in the energy and defense sectors. We are unsurprised and disappointed.

---

During Trump’s second presidency **Russia’s war against Ukraine has become much more deadly**, according to the latest UN report.

[Image]

---

If anyone’s been wondering why Z appears to be bouncing around European capitals like a man possessed, here’s the situation: He’s not stuck between a rock and a hard place. He’s stuck between two rocks and a vacuum.

**Rock One: Russia.** Moscow systematically rejects every proposal—including its own previous demands. Elections? Russia demanded them for years. Z agrees to hold them in 60 days. Russia immediately rejects them and preemptively delegitimizes any Ukrainian government it doesn’t directly control. Energy ceasefire? Rejected. Russia’s too busy destroying the power grid every seven to ten days. Security guarantees? Threats against Europe for even discussing them. The Kremlin’s position is clear: surrender or die slowly.

**Rock Two: America.** For the first time since 1945, the United States has sided with the aggressor in a European war. Trump’s goal isn’t peace—it’s rapprochement with Putin. His officials encouraged Belgium to block access to €210 billion in frozen Russian assets because Washington wants to return them to Russia as part of a settlement. America is actively preventing Europe from funding Ukraine’s defense using Russian money.

**The Vacuum: Europe.** Europe has €245 billion in frozen Russian assets sitting in Belgium. Ukraine needs $200 billion for the next two years. The math is simple. The politics are impossible. Finance ministers worry about the euro’s credibility. Belgium fears Russian retaliation. NATO commanders say 2029 is the deadline for credible deterrence, but Europe can’t replace American capabilities and won’t access the money sitting right there.

So when critics call Z stubborn, what they’re observing is a man trying to play chess while his opponents play calvinball and his supposed allies actively help the other side. His “superhuman stubbornness” isn’t tactical incompetence—it’s the only rational response when every door is locked, every window barred, and the house is on fire.

The parallel universe isn’t Z’s diplomacy. The parallel universe is thinking this was ever going to end in negotiated settlement when one side demands surrender, another wants rapprochement with the aggressor, and the third has the money but won’t spend it.

And yet—somehow—Ukraine hasn’t collapsed. Which tells you everything about Ukrainian resolve and absolutely nothing about Western strategy, because there isn’t one.

> As for the 20 points, there are some positions that still raise questions. In particular, the issue of territory is very sensitive. The Russians want the whole of Donbas, but we obviously cannot accept that. The Americans are looking for some kind of format. They discussed the issue of a ‘free economic zone’. The Americans call it that, while the Russians call it a ‘demilitarised zone’. Our position is that it is fair to stand where we stand, i.e. on the contact line. There is currently a discussion between these positions,” — [Z](https://babel.ua/texts/123664-yakshcho-rosiyani-zalishat-sobi-zaes-vona-ne-bude-pracyuvati-pro-shcho-prezident-rozkazav-zhurnalistam-na-ne-zovsim-zakritiy-zustrichi) (*[Paraphrased by Babel](https://babel.ua/texts/123664-yakshcho-rosiyani-zalishat-sobi-zaes-vona-ne-bude-pracyuvati-pro-shcho-prezident-rozkazav-zhurnalistam-na-ne-zovsim-zakritiy-zustrichi)*, December 12, 2025).

Which brings me to *[The Camp of the Saints](https://archive.org/details/campofsaints00jean/page/6/mode/2up),* which illuminates the worldview of nationalist conservatives now ascendant across democracies. Curtis Yarvin and Renaud Camus discussed this danger over summer, with Yarvin sobbing about the Great Replacement as “one of the greatest crimes” in history—possibly worse than the Holocaust. “I have three children. Will they be basically lined up and marched into mass graves?” This from a man who wrote that “it is Russia’s fate to again restore order in Europe,” who urged Trump to withdraw US forces to signal Putin it’s okay to proceed, who dismisses Ukrainian sovereignty and calls Kyiv and Odesa “great Russian cities.”

> [Curtis Yarvin’s Plot Against America.](https://www.newyorker.com/magazine/2025/06/09/curtis-yarvin-profile) *The reactionary blogger’s call for a monarch to rule the country once seemed like a joke. Now the right is ready to bend the knee* ([The New Yorker](http://newyorker.com), June 2, 2025)

Yarvin connects with Raspail’s worldview as if fed a steady diet of Céline during an Iboga journey. It’s the same intellectual mosh pit Vance, Musk, Thiel, Trump Jr., Bannon, and Hegseth roll around in, oblivious to civic energy.

Z’s playing ping-pong with a wrecking ball. War amplifies core traits. What we’re witnessing is doggedness bordering on self-sacrifice—or perhaps self-immolation. The phase transition, when parallel universes intersect with reality, isn’t theoretical anymore. It’s happening in real time, in the dark, and the angle of impact looks brutal.

## Day 1392 

*Date: 2025-12-16 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Platinum security guarantees*

[Image]

Nothing is agreed except the pretense that it might be.

The Berlin summit’s [joint statement](https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2025/12/15/leaders-statement-on-ukraine/) reads like a Trump golf resort prospectus—lavish promises of security guarantees, peacetime forces of 800,000, European-led multinational battalions, and ceasefire monitoring mechanisms. It’s all very impressive until you notice nobody’s closed on the land yet.

Everyone’s pounding water in a mortar about NATO while treating territorial concessions as existential symbols rather than negotiable assets.

US officials claim “90% consensus” after eight hours of talks. Western leaders “welcome significant progress” and praise “strong convergence” while carefully noting that “some issues would need to be resolved in the final stages.” German Chancellor Merz calls America’s commitments “truly remarkable.” Translation: we’ve agreed on everything except the one thing that matters. That pesky 10% happens to include whether Ukraine surrenders the the defensive line protecting the country since 2014—a “demilitarized economic zone” where Russia gets sovereignty but pinky-swears not to occupy it with troops.

[ISW](https://understandingwar.org/research/russia-ukraine/russian-offensive-campaign-assessment-december-15-2025/)’s toplines say officials agreed on “NATO-like” security guarantees—but the Kremlin has consistently rejected any deal offering *reliable* Ukrainian security.

Trump’s team promises Article 5-like guarantees, with Senate votes and everything. They’ll help Ukraine maintain 800,000 troops, secure its skies, provide intelligence and logistics. The only catch? No ground troops. And that demilitarized zone business. Oh, and Russia has to agree—which Moscow has already said it won’t, especially to those security guarantees.

The Berlin statement ends with a telling line: “It is now incumbent upon Russia to show willingness to work towards lasting peace.” But Russia’s already shown its willingness—by declining every negotiation, rejecting every framework, and continuing to shell Ukrainian cities.

Nothing is agreed until everything is agreed, the statement reminds us. Quite right. And right now, nothing is agreed except the pretense that it might be.

## Day 1393 

*Date: 2025-12-17 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Waiting for Russia*

[Image]

They meet. They talk. They draft documents. Two documents. Perhaps three. The documents say things. Specific things. Very concrete things, they say. But what things? They cannot say. The things remain unsaid.

Belgium waits. Belgium wants guarantees. A blank check, they say. For forever, they say. The others hesitate. Perhaps they will give the check. Perhaps not. 50:50, says Merz. Perhaps less. They will decide Thursday. Or perhaps not Thursday. Perhaps the weekend.

There are €210 billion. Most in Belgium. Some in France. Smaller amounts elsewhere. The billions wait. Frozen. Immobilized. But not seized. Not yet. Perhaps never. They want to use them. They cannot use them. Russia has filed suit. $230 billion they demand. The circle closes.

Eighteen countries have treaties. Bilateral treaties with Russia. They should rescind them. They do not rescind them. The treaties wait. Russia could use them. Will use them. Through investor-state mechanisms. The words pile up. Nothing happens.

France demands Ukraine buy only European weapons. Not American weapons. European weapons. The others consider this. They draft more documents. Operating documents. Mil-to-mil documents. Eight hours of talks in Berlin. About a dozen countries. Top officials. They sign off, mostly. Progress, they say. Real and concrete progress.

800,000 troops for Ukraine. A peacetime level. Sustained and significant support required. European forces on the ground. Not NATO forces. Just European forces. In western Ukraine. Away from the cease-fire line. Which line? There is no line. There is no cease-fire. Russia is not party to these talks. Russia demands the Donbas. Russia forbids NATO troops. Russia shows no flexibility at all.

Trump says no American troops. Just intelligence. Vast intelligence systems. To monitor. To verify. To detect false flags. The Americans will lead a mechanism. International participation. Early warning. Of what? Of future attacks. Which attacks? After which cease-fire? Which cease-fire exactly?

The Budapest Memorandum failed in 2014. Failed again in 2022. This time will be different, they say. Legally binding, they say. Subject to procedures. Trump will submit to Senate. Perhaps as a treaty. Perhaps not. They do not say.

Z expects finalization. In the coming days. Perhaps this weekend. Then convey to Russia. Then meet with negotiators. Then perhaps. Perhaps then.

The Germans speak. Pistorius says the plan is good in its core. Now we wait for Moscow’s reaction, he says. Laschet says we’ve never been this close to peace. Whether it leads to ceasefire remains open, he says. Depends on Moscow’s reaction, he says. Klingbeil says we shouldn’t take the fifth step before the first. We are already the greatest supporter, he says. We will always meet our responsibility, he says. When the debate really arises, he says.

The Kremlin speaks. No Christmas ceasefire, they say. No details received on guarantees, they say. Putin must say where the journey goes, the Germans say. Then we will see further, they say.

The documents wait. The billions wait. Thursday waits. Russia does not wait. Russia is not waiting. Russia was never waiting.

The siren goes. Again.

## Day 1400

*Date: 2025-12-24 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*The 20-point peace plan*

Welcome to Toretsk—population was 31,000, give or take whoever's still breathing. Pokrovsk, Kostiantynivka, and Myrnohrad—slightly bigger towns at 60,000, 67,000, and 46,000 respectively—are going for the same 'liberated' aesthetic Russia's so fond of advertising.

Russians uploaded this video recently from inside Pokrovsk.

### Peace talks

Here, finally, is the framework of the peace plan, as per Z:

> **Draft Peace Plan: 20-Point Overview**
>
> **Sovereignty & Security (Points 1-6)**
>
> - Reaffirms Ukraine’s sovereignty
> - Full non-aggression agreement with Russia, monitored via satellite surveillance
> - Strong security guarantees for Ukraine
> - Ukrainian Armed Forces maintained at 800,000 troops in peacetime
> - U.S., NATO, and EU provide Article 5-style security guarantees
> - Russia must enshrine non-aggression policy toward Europe and Ukraine in law
>
> **Economic & European Integration (Points 7-10)**
>
> - Ukraine joins EU on specific timeline with short-term privileged market access
> - Major development package covering tech, AI, data centers, and gas infrastructure
> - Multiple funds created for economic restoration and humanitarian issues
> - Free trade agreement with the United States
>
> **Nuclear & Energy (Points 11-12)**
>
> - Ukraine remains non-nuclear per NPT
> - Zaporizhia nuclear plant jointly operated by Ukraine, U.S., and Russia (no compromise reached yet)
>
> **Social & Territorial (Points 13-16)**
>
> - Both sides implement tolerance education programs; Ukraine adopts EU rules on religious tolerance and minority language protection
> - **Most difficult point:** Current troop deployment lines in Donetsk, Luhansk, Zaporizhia, and Kherson become de facto contact line; Russia must withdraw from Dnipropetrovsk, Mykolaiv, Sumy, and Kharkiv regions for agreement to take effect
> - No future territorial changes by force
> - Russia allows Ukraine commercial use of Dnipro River and Black Sea; Kinburn Spit demilitarized
>
> **Implementation (Points 17-20)**
>
> - All-for-all POW exchange (including those convicted since 2014); return of all civilian detainees, hostages, children, and political prisoners
> - Ukraine holds elections as soon as possible after signing
> - Peace Council chaired by Trump monitors implementation; includes Ukraine, Europe, NATO, Russia, and U.S.; sanctions for violations
> - Ceasefire takes effect immediately upon agreement

[Image]

How charmingly both delegations describe their Florida soirée—”productive,” “constructive,” all those reassuring adjectives deployed like diplomatic sandbags. Witkoff’s [memo](https://x.com/SEPeaceMissions/status/2002826320615444873?s=20) reads like a corporate retreat agenda: four key documents, timelines discussed, boxes ticked. One almost expects a PowerPoint on synergistic peace frameworks.

Z, meanwhile, delivers his Christmas address amid 635 Russian drones and 38 missiles targeting energy infrastructure—the ninth such large-scale strike since January. He insists Ukraine “will never be an obstacle to peace” while noting Russia must take ending the war “100% seriously.” This, as Russian mechanized columns of 24 armored vehicles get obliterated near Pokrovsk in daylight assaults so incompetent even Russian milbloggers are complaining.

The gap between Mar-a-Lago’s strategic planning sessions and Ukraine’s smoldering power stations represents not merely a diplomatic challenge but a category error. One side drafts 20-point plans; the other launches Christmas strikes. Guess which approach Putin finds more persuasive?

## Day 1401 

*Date: 2025-12-25 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*The twenty-point plan*

[Image]

Melting coldness around a heart with human warmth: this year’s ice sculpture.

[Image]

[Image]

[Image]

I don’t know what to make of the 20-point plan semi-agreed between Team Ukraine and Team USA, with Team Europe’s help.

The problem with armchair strategizing is that it treats survival as an intellectual exercise, a game of hypothetical scenarios where “time” becomes an abstract asset to be “used wisely.”

There is no “wise use of time” when your negotiating partner’s opening position is your non-existence. This is not an exercise in understanding nuance. It’s more about who pays the price for compromising principles and appeasement.

## Day 1402 

*Date: 2025-12-26 | Status: Published | Audience: only_paid*

*Odesa's cold winter*

[Image]

Yes, it’s a bottle of Tequila. Lokita Blanco. Very good.

Dusting of snow this morning.

Kim Barker has a lovely article w/ pictures about how miserable life is in Odesa this winter, especially for the elderly and disabled[1](#footnote-1).

Z in his Christmas Day pep talk said he and his team talked for an hour with Steven Witkoff and Jared Kushner about next steps and the timing of peace framework plans. Putin will def not agree to any framework that might restore Ukraine’s viability after the fighting stops. Talking continues irregardless of this fact.

Parties to the conflict are now discussing DMZs, zones of influence, ADBZs, free trade zones, etc., presidential elections, referendums, etc. For this there are at least two reasons: desperation and a complete absence of options. Some try to counteract these by crusting proposals in security guarantees, or by cramming them with prosperity roadmaps, or by floating them in demilitarized zones, but these solutions—like reckless bling bling or a bald man’s rug—do little more than shine light on the problem.

The latest 20-point plan differs from November’s 28-pointer in that it’s marginally less suicidal: 800,000 peacetime troops instead of 600,000, a freeze along current lines rather than withdrawal from unoccupied Donbas. The Kremlin wants complete withdrawal from all four oblasts, denazification, demilitarization that ensures Ukraine cannot defend itself, and international recognition of annexations. Unplug the smoke detector, because this gap isn’t closing.

Ukrainian society treats any compromise as absolute capitulation—the difference between Versailles and unconditional surrender becomes meaningless when you’re convinced the next bite comes regardless. However, there is a gap between “first stage” and “second stage” where things could go completely off the rails. But total disintegration requires time, which requires a deal, which requires Putin to accept something less than total subjugation. Check for logic: there isn’t any.

Meanwhile Ukrainian forces have liberated 182 square kilometers around Kupyansk since December 11, with Russian milbloggers now admitting their command lied about controlling eleven settlements when they actually held three. The military reality keeps not cooperating with anyone’s peace frameworks.

[Image]

Reduce the remaining delusions over high heat until there’s about half a cup, then check again for salt and pepper. Serve the contradiction in a gravy boat, presumably with a slightly perverse look on your face.

Have a great weekend!
